{% raw %}The author's Substack profile was impersonated by a scammer (@locarmenn) who followed subscribers attempting to deceive people Musicians' identities are increasingly being stolen for romance scams, catfishing, and fraudulent impersonation on social media platforms Historical and contemporary examples of identity theft include fake Bruno Mars profiles that scammed a woman out of $100k, fake guitar websites, and AI-generated music theft targeting indie artists The 1968 case of fake Zombies bands created by a Michigan booking agent is explored, including how two members went on to form ZZ Top AI identity theft scams are now targeting the indie music community, with artists unable to prove ownership or remove false copyright claims{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}Apple Explores Shaking Up Its Smartwatch for a New Era{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/apple-explores-shaking-up-its-smartwatch-for-a-new-era-d3a27a/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="apple-explores-shaking-up-its-smartwatch-for-a-new-era-d3a27a"></span>

{% raw %}The Apple Watch continues to attract new buyers and remains the top-selling smartwatch. However, the company's industrial design team has been exploring a broad rethink of Apple's smartwatches and is evaluating several different directions. Apple needs to adapt to the rise of screenless fitness bands and smart rings, which are cheaper, lighter, and more fun and convenient to use for many people. The company aims to inject new life into its Wearables, Home, and Accessories segment, which has struggled to generate meaningful growth in recent years.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}Peak Energy, a US-based startup, has raised tens of millions of dollars to build a factory for its sodium-ion battery packs. It already has more than $1.1 billion in announced deals with customers. Sodium batteries have less-intensive cooling needs than lithium-ion packs, and they are less likely to catch fire. Another US-based startup, Inlyte Energy, is working on another type of sodium-based battery that can't catch fire at all.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}Google's recent reshuffling of its AI operations has many thinking that the company has failed in the AI race. However, the company may just be refocusing its resources on its strengths. Google Cloud may be a much larger economic opportunity than pursuing its rivals in the frontier AI race. The company's enormous distribution advantage means it doesn't have to compete in the expensive and unprofitable frontier race.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}While other tech giants struggle to justify their AI spending, Shopify is seeing major benefits. {% raw %}Nvidia has partnered with six large asset managers on a $500 billion financing push designed to treat compute infrastructure much like commercial real estate, toll roads, or other assets to borrow against. The effort will mobilize third-party capital for hyperscalers, frontier AI labs, and enterprises to build out data centers and acquire Nvidia hardware. It will help Nvidia's customers secure financing without tapping their own balance sheets. Nvidia believes lenders can reliably underwrite compute as a revenue-generating asset with an extended life as its hardware is broadly adopted and transferable across customers.{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}Fix LengthValidator crash with proc minimum and nil value{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/fix-lengthvalidator-crash-with-proc-minimum-and-nil-value-284a07/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="fix-lengthvalidator-crash-with-proc-minimum-and-nil-value-284a07"></span>
_uloza+hey@proton.me · [unsubscribe](https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}The proc was resolved only when the value was present, so for a nil value it leaked unresolved into the error message and was invoked with the message options hash instead of the record. It is now resolved before the error is built, producing the expected "is too short" message.{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}Append TRADITIONAL instead of STRICT_ALL_TABLES to MySQL's sql_mode by default{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/append-traditional-instead-of-strict-all-tables-to-mysql-s-s-1cc46c/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="append-traditional-instead-of-strict-all-tables-to-mysql-s-s-1cc46c"></span>
_uloza+hey@proton.me · [unsubscribe](https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}On environments whose global sql_mode is empty — most notably Amazon RDS and Aurora MySQL default parameter groups — appending only STRICT_ALL_TABLES leaves out NO_ZERO_IN_DATE, NO_ZERO_DATE, and ERROR_FOR_DIVISION_BY_ZERO, which MySQL 5.7+ has otherwise made part of its own default. Appending TRADITIONAL closes the gap.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/append-traditional-instead-of-strict-all-tables-to-mysql-s-s-1cc46c/)

### [{% raw %}Change the shape of ActiveRecord::Migration::CommandRecorder#commands{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/change-the-shape-of-activerecord-migration-commandrecorder-c-050359/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="change-the-shape-of-activerecord-migration-commandrecorder-c-050359"></span>
_uloza+hey@proton.me · [unsubscribe](https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}Each recorded migration command is now stored as [cmd, args, kwargs, block] (4-element) instead of [cmd, args, block] (3-element) with kwargs bundled into a trailing hash inside args. Code that inspects recorder.commands directly needs to adapt to the new tuple shape.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/change-the-shape-of-activerecord-migration-commandrecorder-c-050359/)

### [{% raw %}Fix normalizes to run before the underlying type validates an assigned value{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/fix-normalizes-to-run-before-the-underlying-type-validates-a-402273/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="fix-normalizes-to-run-before-the-underlying-type-validates-a-402273"></span>
_uloza+hey@proton.me · [unsubscribe](https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}When normalizes was combined with another type that rejects invalid input (such as an Active Record enum), the underlying type's assert_valid_value ran against the raw, un-normalized value and raised before normalization had a chance to run. The normalization is now applied first, so a value like " Pending " is normalized to "pending" and accepted by the enum.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/fix-normalizes-to-run-before-the-underlying-type-validates-a-402273/)

### [{% raw %}Negative-interest tech debt{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/negative-interest-tech-debt-5eec93/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="negative-interest-tech-debt-5eec93"></span>
_Leadership in Tech · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}Tech debt is usually prioritized by how fast it compounds, but Jesse points out that AI may be changing that calculation. If AI keeps improving, fixing messy code in the future could be easier than fixing it today, even after more code has piled on top. That makes the effective interest rate on tech debt potentially negative, which would mean shipping fast now beats cleaning up later. Companies are already betting on this, though nobody yet knows if the bet will pay off.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/negative-interest-tech-debt-5eec93/)

### [{% raw %}Improving heuristics{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/improving-heuristics-94e479/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="improving-heuristics-94e479"></span>
_Programming Digest · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}Placing "landmark" nodes on a map can make A* run faster without changing the algorithm itself. Before each search, Amit suggests running Dijkstra's from each landmark and storing the costs. Then use the triangle inequality to get a tighter lower bound on path length, replacing the standard distance heuristic. Placing more landmarks near map edges or common paths means fewer nodes are explored, though the best placement depends on your specific game and map layout.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/improving-heuristics-94e479/)

### [{% raw %}A deep (and fuzzy) dive into search{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/a-deep-and-fuzzy-dive-into-search-e99852/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="a-deep-and-fuzzy-dive-into-search-e99852"></span>
_Programming Digest · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}Fuzzy search helps search engines find results even when words contain typos or extra characters. Andrey explains two approaches used by SereneDB: Levenshtein automata and n-gram similarity. Levenshtein is useful for precise typo matching, while n-grams work better with longer text and partial matches. SereneDB combines these techniques to make fuzzy search practical and flexible.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/a-deep-and-fuzzy-dive-into-search-e99852/)

{% raw %}Filip introduces shell event designators, a powerful but often overlooked way to reuse and modify previous commands. He explains useful shortcuts such as !!, !$, and !ssh, along with word and path modifiers. Filip also covers the POSIX fc command, safer ways to use these features, and when alternatives like Ctrl-R may be better.{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}Email encryption{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/email-encryption-6355f7/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="email-encryption-6355f7"></span>
_Programming Digest · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}Email was invented in many places at once, and its security was never a priority. Two main encryption standards emerged: PGP, which is open and decentralized but notoriously hard to use, and S/MIME, which works well inside organizations with shared infrastructure but struggles across them. In practice, most email today is encrypted in transit between servers, which solves many real threats even without full end-to-end encryption. The core problem is that strong encryption requires key distribution, and no universal solution for that has ever taken hold.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/email-encryption-6355f7/)

{% raw %}The idea of the dark factory promises software development with little or no human involvement. But the teams closest to this work tell a more nuanced story. {% raw %}Generative UI replaces walls of AI text with dynamic interface elements that adapt to what users actually want. Three approaches exist: static components mapped from tool results, declarative JSON specs, and open-ended code generation inside sandboxed iframes. These are not mutually exclusive, and production apps will likely combine all three.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}Existing benchmarks let models pass every test while still writing React code that breaks in production. {% raw %}Anthropic's Claude tested on Riemann Hypothesis increased share of zeta zeros on critical line from 41.6% to 67.2%, a 25.6% gain in 36 hours vs. 0.8% over 37 years by humans Achievement required brute force stamina: 650 failed attempts across 60 agents and 31 million tokens, not novel mathematical insight Represents broader trend of AI breaking decades-old mathematical records through persistence rather than genius{% endraw %}

{% raw %}Mark Zuckerberg recently released a wide-ranging essay that outlined a new course of action to spread the wealth and opportunity from AI to users around the world, and to residents of the communities that host the data centers powering it. Zuckerberg's plans include releasing more open-weight models and establishing a $1 billion fund to invest in the communities where Meta hosts data centers. He believes that Meta's approach to developing powerful AI and distributing it as widely as possible is the least likely path to disastrous outcomes for humanity.{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}Learning more about Claude's mathematical capabilities{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/learning-more-about-claude-s-mathematical-capabilities-0b3b75/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="learning-more-about-claude-s-mathematical-capabilities-0b3b75"></span>
_aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=19a357d8-9567-11f1-8920-5509caa85f4b%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786442421%26t=1786446300%26s=fc247acc8bb43bac5f03a6bb2beab7cfcd8e4075f86c22f936cd2becadffed74/1/0100019ff07f45ea-2a2ade7c-c4d8-499e-ac2b-8e5de6023415-000000/rWP3qNiD0yXK-XrlRacLGrx494EiqNaC8XEZotRbtE0=452)_

{% raw %}Claude was recently challenged with one of the most famous unsolved problems in mathematics: the Riemann hypothesis. While Claude didn't succeed, it unexpectedly made strides on a related problem. It improved on a longstanding lower bound for the fraction of zeros of the Riemann zeta function that satisfy the Riemann hypothesis. The result shows that AI models like Claude can extend the impact and reach of mathematicians' ideas in new and sometimes surprising ways.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/learning-more-about-claude-s-mathematical-capabilities-0b3b75/)

### [{% raw %}No, local models will not win{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/no-local-models-will-not-win-0f812e/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="no-local-models-will-not-win-0f812e"></span>
_aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=19a357d8-9567-11f1-8920-5509caa85f4b%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786442421%26t=1786446300%26s=fc247acc8bb43bac5f03a6bb2beab7cfcd8e4075f86c22f936cd2becadffed74/1/0100019ff07f45ea-2a2ade7c-c4d8-499e-ac2b-8e5de6023415-000000/rWP3qNiD0yXK-XrlRacLGrx494EiqNaC8XEZotRbtE0=452)_

{% raw %}Local models are never going to be as powerful as datacenter models. All of the current frontier models are far too big to run on anything but a full GPU cluster. Smaller models are getting more intelligent, but datacenter models will always be the strongest available. When given a choice, people are going to pick the strongest available model in their price range, the one that frustrates them least, which is always going to be the bigger, more powerful one.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/no-local-models-will-not-win-0f812e/)

### [{% raw %}AI Pattern Makes Toyota Invisible to Camera{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/ai-pattern-makes-toyota-invisible-to-camera-ea2eed/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="ai-pattern-makes-toyota-invisible-to-camera-ea2eed"></span>
_MyClaw Newsletter · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=98c25587-e84c-44c2-84de-1f862d1ace82)_

{% raw %}Cybersecurity researcher Bill Swearingen wrapped a Toyota Yaris in an AI-generated adversarial pattern and drove it past a Flock surveillance camera at Def Con. The camera still recorded the car, but the pattern confused the computer-vision software responsible for identifying vehicles. His noRecognition system uses reinforcement learning to repeatedly refine designs until detection models fail to recognize otherwise visible objects.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}OpenAI, Anthropic and Meta all referenced Irregular after recent AI security incidents because the Israeli startup ran the independent evaluation environments used to test their frontier models. ### [{% raw %}Agentic Code Quality{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/agentic-code-quality-378aa6/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agentic-code-quality-378aa6"></span>
_Pointer · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aiYGihRTwZYkR0HjoovPrejageBVLzHH9tG8n84m3dFIuMnCMDviQfRQLGnrFY4wdh3h9Zwffqoap7IahStn1Zx9FxBC0dbqtZcIWs5Qb2lN3W7NPzwNLQCfjXO_oDddTMOMO_mDbiK4VXjp1fg0-PLD5T9qz5DNPZ4gPMhln17AcwX5HCUm5nV8yifu3QO6FA3jpiAkxgp2Mga6WTGfDYbfAq_T7sK9zfaJAm00tC2QunGj2lY86lBE1dFZp5U6N_im05m5AIB2IAgDvWKJ3YhaB56b4WVanjznLY5Bvo_orK3zGNNsqe9Fz-h354g125n0GK31bnyYRICxTARq7LIQcIbxkh2-GC2sY0FTySAcgNi5v83clGhv8d5LZFgU6MZAxYta1vlgcYL0Y25YRfZ3yYWPPDI-LWTVEBF2_6tzVavgtSBxtb7oLFpmuv_cQ0c9fh6G6tostqN1n6B_0dRwcLo59JmH6XsbaIW-96oRi4PTJpCTy5avyoqVYEgUPMgUD9IoCGpOrlGLE8xP5OmnrjFk-gxKhBberFaQhbUA_3gZN0jQBfqMwHzbKhl0Prhr8kw71jWOxARr4om1Dh67yfV6bUA3OTNRS6Q-1cAWi9DGrOKMhpYg-sRiHIeOpfMmJULlis7grAKzRUCNZL9hDIrWT67t46do1V3Y0Y9fpuV_5fbXsSJtWcl3JdKIVEa_6bhhpDeVb3q_yuzXYEsCKii1OeVAiNU9Tsd_KCB9dbJ8WXE_DrLc5E5-4pnRYCQhyHJJ-IrjWYwpGens3VKw/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h30/h001.ufyGOYgPPqykKadV2GndXMR7RdGBVK_zskIjnMjfT7M)_

{% raw %}For much of human history, we've evaluated code quality via code review: someone reads what you wrote and makes sure it's clean, thoughtful, fast, understandable, and tests well. For agents, that approach doesn't scale well; there's just too much code for anyone to read. As a result, more and more of our quality checks have to happen in the harness, environment, and operating system around the agent. Software quality now depends on the constraints you set around your agents.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}AI lets people do tasks they couldn't before, but there's a catch: your ability to do something can now outrun your ability to judge whether it's good. ### [{% raw %}Compression is prediction{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/compression-is-prediction-b5d363/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="compression-is-prediction-b5d363"></span>
_Programming Digest · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}Compression works by finding patterns in data and encoding them more efficiently, and better predictions about what comes next lead to better compression. Language models like GPT work the same way: they predict the next word based on context, which is exactly what a compression model does. A 2023 DeepMind paper confirmed this link, showing the two are mathematically equivalent. The main reason we don't use LLMs for everyday compression is cost — they are far too large and slow for practical use.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}Promising career rewards to keep team members happy often backfires badly. The initial boost fades fast, and if the promise falls through, trust is shattered and can take years to rebuild. ### [{% raw %}Engineering management after the cost of code collapsed{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/engineering-management-after-the-cost-of-code-collapsed-f05eac/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="engineering-management-after-the-cost-of-code-collapsed-f05eac"></span>

{% raw %}Engineering management rules aren't uniformly obsolete or valid in the AI era. The key is checking what assumption each practice rests on. If a rule depends on the cost of writing code, revisit it. If it depends on how humans coordinate, verify, or accept risk, it still holds. Code generation got cheap, but specification, judgment, and ownership did not. Whoever has to sign a decision still sets the pace.{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}When nothing happens{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/when-nothing-happens-a30c66/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="when-nothing-happens-a30c66"></span>
_Leadership in Tech · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}Organizations don't punish bad behavior — they punish bad behavior when results turn. Research shows that whether a harsh leader is seen as inspiring or abusive depends mostly on whether the team is winning. Cases like Steve Jobs, Linda Wachner, and Bobby Knight all follow the same pattern: the behavior stays constant, but the moment results drop, tolerance evaporates. The real question for any leader is how much cover strong performance is currently buying for someone on their team.{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}React 19 useActionState explained{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/react-19-useactionstate-explained-13ee5f/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="react-19-useactionstate-explained-13ee5f"></span>
_React Digest · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://reactdigest.net/subscribers/15ba1f04-697f-45f3-a1e0-b46f193a8e06/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}React 19's useActionState hook replaces the repetitive pattern of manually managing form submission state. Pass it an action function and an initial state, and it returns the current state, a form action, and a pending flag. The biggest production mistake is using a local pending flag instead of the built-in one, which can let users queue duplicate submissions. Shubhra suggests skipping it for optimistic updates, multi-step wizards, or anything not tied to a form.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}AI coding tools often suggest outdated frontend patterns because their training data lags behind the web platform. Google Chrome's modern-web-guidance tool addresses this by letting you search a curated set of best-practice guides before writing code. Tested against a real React app, it caught missing dark mode declarations, forms lacking native submission handling, and stale validation approaches, while correctly flagging which existing code was already sound. Its most useful feature is tying browser support advice to concrete Baseline dates, so you know exactly when a pattern is safe to ship without a fallback.{% endraw %}

It uses selectors and stable actions to reduce unnecessary re-renders while keeping React's familiar hooks and context model.{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}Experimenting with RSCs for performance and UX in Next.js{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/experimenting-with-rscs-for-performance-and-ux-in-next-js-a6c43e/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="experimenting-with-rscs-for-performance-and-ux-in-next-js-a6c43e"></span>
_React Digest · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://reactdigest.net/subscribers/15ba1f04-697f-45f3-a1e0-b46f193a8e06/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}Aurora explores how React Server Components can improve performance and user experience in Next.js. She shows how to keep data fetching and rendering on the server while using small client components for interaction. Her examples include loading more posts, streaming search results, and server-rendered message previews. She also explains how URLs, Suspense, and Server Functions can create fast, responsive interfaces.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/experimenting-with-rscs-for-performance-and-ux-in-next-js-a6c43e/)

### [{% raw %}Making referential stability a type{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/making-referential-stability-a-type-08e5d5/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="making-referential-stability-a-type-08e5d5"></span>
_React Digest · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://reactdigest.net/subscribers/15ba1f04-697f-45f3-a1e0-b46f193a8e06/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}Inline arrays and callbacks passed to memoized components cause silent re-renders that are hard to catch. Jovi's experiment adds a branded type called Stable that marks a reference as guaranteed to survive unrelated renders. Strict wrappers for React and Preact hooks then reject unstable dependencies at the exact line where they appear, with an error message explaining how to fix it. The goal is to move that contract from comments and lint rules into the type system itself.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/making-referential-stability-a-type-08e5d5/)

### [{% raw %}We stopped using RSC on TanStack{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/we-stopped-using-rsc-on-tanstack-dfd6db/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="we-stopped-using-rsc-on-tanstack-dfd6db"></span>
_React Digest · via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://reactdigest.net/subscribers/15ba1f04-697f-45f3-a1e0-b46f193a8e06/unsubscribe)_

{% raw %}TanStack stopped using React Server Components after making its markdown and code-rendering tools much smaller. React Server Components had improved performance by keeping large dependencies off the client, but it also added architectural complexity. With smaller packages, regular server-side rendering became simpler while keeping the site fast. The experience suggests React Server Components is useful for heavy server-side work, but it should not be used when simpler solutions are enough.{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/we-stopped-using-rsc-on-tanstack-dfd6db/)

{% raw %}NASA's Perseverance Mars rover will set a record for the most distance driven by any vehicle on another world sometime next week. The rover's auto navigation system has enabled about 90% of the distance driven by the vehicle to be autonomous. Not having to wait for navigation commands from drivers on Earth has allowed the rover to maximize the amount of scientific return. The vehicle will likely be able to keep driving itself across Mars for many years to come.{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}Agentic Code Quality{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/agentic-code-quality-25030b/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agentic-code-quality-25030b"></span>
_aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=f6b93b62-9498-11f1-b407-617d207c3f6a%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786356070%26t=1786359902%26s=74af2209c230a330bc89fc8a8548c43ac279017a75ea47abb8646f99d405931b/1/0100019feb58f02b-b9cd628c-a490-43d3-8cb6-db5f7e5b0340-000000/psYd67HjVJrQUzip8J0z9-_IyH9_mJ4GMRMtx76jGhI=452)_

{% raw %}Software quality now depends on the constraints set around the agents that wrote it. Constraints define what a system is allowed to do. Setting and maintaining these constraints allows developers to build loops that reliably deliver high-quality production software. Agents can propose anything - constraints decide whether a proposal is safe enough, correct, scoped, and useful.{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}Changing Devtools Is Cheap. Owning Them Isn't{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/changing-devtools-is-cheap-owning-them-isn-t-50a1ab/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="changing-devtools-is-cheap-owning-them-isn-t-50a1ab"></span>
_aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=f6b93b62-9498-11f1-b407-617d207c3f6a%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786356070%26t=1786359902%26s=74af2209c230a330bc89fc8a8548c43ac279017a75ea47abb8646f99d405931b/1/0100019feb58f02b-b9cd628c-a490-43d3-8cb6-db5f7e5b0340-000000/psYd67HjVJrQUzip8J0z9-_IyH9_mJ4GMRMtx76jGhI=452)_

{% raw %}The future of software will be powered by high-quality, well-documented building blocks that users can build specialized artifacts on top of. Agents are good at composing high-quality components. Maintainers can provide these components alongside a focus application, and users can choose how to modify their experiences themselves. This allows software to be personalized while still being developed by maintainers that care.{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}What's the best programming language for coding agents?{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/what-s-the-best-programming-language-for-coding-agents-93cee7/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="what-s-the-best-programming-language-for-coding-agents-93cee7"></span>
_aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com · [unsubscribe](https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=19a357d8-9567-11f1-8920-5509caa85f4b%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786442421%26t=1786446300%26s=fc247acc8bb43bac5f03a6bb2beab7cfcd8e4075f86c22f936cd2becadffed74/1/0100019ff07f45ea-2a2ade7c-c4d8-499e-ac2b-8e5de6023415-000000/rWP3qNiD0yXK-XrlRacLGrx494EiqNaC8XEZotRbtE0=452)_

{% raw %}Anyone who wants to make a strong claim about which language or classes of languages are particularly good with LLMs would need to run quite a few different evaluations. Failures with languages are generally something idiosyncratic, where it's not always obvious how much the issue generalizes across tasks or setups. There's no way to look at the score on one or even several evaluations and draw a conclusion about programming in general. At this point, it would be a mistake to draw a strong conclusion about any particular language.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}Gen Z doesn't have much in the way of savings, but it does love to spend on little treats. Zoomers burn cash on beauty products, snacks, and jewelry. Economists are calling it the 'little treat economy,' the result of joyful splurging on frivolous purchases to fill the void left by unaffordable big-ticket items, like homes. Gen Z's average spending on little treats grew by more than 6% YoY in June, compared to 3% for all generations.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}The conditions for Elon Musk to receive his $1 trillion pay package at Tesla could easily be achieved if the company were acquired. There is a clause in the agreement that declares half the targets as good as accomplished if Tesla is acquired or otherwise taken over. This leaves only market-value targets to determine how many shares Musk gets. Musk has considerable say over any offer SpaceX makes for Tesla. Even a stratospheric bid would keep Musk in firm control of the combined company.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}Dyna-2 is a world-action model (WAM) pre-trained on over one million hours of human video data. Its existence proves that scaling human video data predictably improves zero-shot performance on unseen robot hardware. Dyna-2 achieved an 87% pass rate in real-world zero-shot deployments at customer sites, vastly outperforming the 46% pass rate of its VLA predecessor, Dyna-1. The model uses a new one-step video generation distillation pipeline that drops inference latency by two orders of magnitude for downstream planning.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}AI writes more code than ever. Reviewing it shouldn't mean scrolling forty files in alphabetical order. CodeRabbit Review reorganizes any pull request from a flat file list into a structured, layer-by-layer walkthrough - the logical reading order of the change, not the order your platform happens to sort it. Every range gets its own plain-language summary, with sequence diagrams, state machines, and ERDs generated inline wherever a visual earns its place. (1) Cohorts group related files and chunks so you review one idea at a time. (2) Layers order them so foundational changes - data shapes, contracts - come before the code that depends on them. (3) Code Peek lets you click any variable, function, class or type to see its definition and usages without leaving the tab. (4) Semantic Diff cuts past formatting noise to show what actually changed. From the team that pioneered AI code reviews. 2M reviews every week. 6M repos. 15K customers. Free during early access.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}(1) If you want to help people learn something, look into making it engaging / entertaining. This first one is the easier, more obvious conclusion. (2) It is actually not in your best interest to be the kind of person who can only learn from entertaining content. Nor to be the kind of person that needs any sort of entertainment as a prerequisite for learning. The vast majority of smart people will never consider this second conclusion.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}If you're willing to embrace this fact that a little bit of time in middle management roles is important preparation to become an executive, but spending a great deal of time in middle management only prepares you for further middle management roles–and makes you less effective as a potential executive–then the AI-driven shift in managerial fads might be a threat to your current role, but it's likely to improve your chances to succeed as an executive.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}Agents hit your signup flow and bounce off a login built for humans. Every one that gives up is a signup you never see. WorkOS Agent Registration turns that traffic into signups. Enroll via the dashboard and AuthKit publishes an auth.md file agents read to register for scoped, short-lived credentials you control.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}There's a lot of buzz that AI means companies only want seniors now. That there's no room left for early career engineers. I don't think that's true. The thing actually worth worrying about isn't whether you'll get hired, it's whether you keep learning in a world of AI, now that it removes the friction where that learning used to happen. And that, thankfully, is a choice you get to make.{% endraw %}

{% raw %}This series is about making your programs more resilient. Each post looks at one part of the system we take for granted: networks, time, databases, storage, users, dependencies, deployments, queues, and concurrency. The question is always the same: what assumptions are we making here, and what should we do when they stop being true?{% endraw %}

{% raw %}A lot of it is generic and does not provide practical insight to use, still, one thing that is important to get comfortable (and learn to find balance) with is this: "There is a genuine pressure to get things done quickly, because there are always 10 more things in the pipeline waiting to get done."{% endraw %}

### [{% raw %}Using Agents Is Literally Engineering Management{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/using-agents-is-literally-engineering-management-b9430d/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="using-agents-is-literally-engineering-management-b9430d"></span>
_uloza+hey@proton.me · [unsubscribe](https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=22ba65c4f8)_

{% raw %}Wonderful post by Jack Danger with a great take that truly captures the early days in managing humans and AI: "A first-time manager cuts hard toward either micromanaging or abdication... That's the IC-and-AI spectrum right now. Either you hand-hold every line of output, or you YOLO it to production and hope. [...] let yourself accept that all of the models and all of the people are imperfect. Then commit to building the skills."{% endraw %}

[Read full piece →](/2026/08/16/stories/using-agents-is-literally-engineering-management-b9430d/)

### [{% raw %}A Fresh Definition of the Product Role{% endraw %}](/2026/08/16/stories/a-fresh-definition-of-the-product-role-7bfa42/) <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="a-fresh-definition-of-the-product-role-7bfa42"></span>

{% raw %}If the cost of building is lower, the value of knowing what to build is much higher. The problem is that many product managers think they're great because they can articulate what to build. Great PMs can deeply articulate why to build: "the hard part is knowing that it should exist, and knowing how it should exist."{% endraw %}
